`TrainController.get_lookahead()` returns the canonical lookahead payload (the `Dict` shape defined in **canonical lookahead payload**). `TrainController.lookahead` is the per-block `LookaheadPoint` list projection over the same computation. Both are computed each time they are read, from `(current_span, cab, received_path_plan, network_state, signals, current_speed_kmh, eb.active)`. `received_path_plan` supplies the ordered authorized corridor. Speed limits, block/switch state, signal-derived diagnostics, and physical position are resolved from the live network/controller state, not copied into the controller authority payload. There is no stored authority-payload field, and no separate "pre-physics" / "post-physics" copies; every read returns the live result. A within-tick memo keyed on `(world_version, head, cab, eb.active)` may cache the computed result across reads inside a single tick, as an implementation optimization, as long as it is discarded before the next tick and is shadow-verified identical to a live recompute, so every caller still sees the live result. Persistent / cross-tick projection caching with an invalidation surface stays Out of Scope; see the **Out of Scope** section.

**Controller state machine**

**`READY` state** (referenced elsewhere by name): the controller's `READY` state means powered, stopped, not dwelling, regardless of cab selection. Other sites must cross-reference `state == READY` and must not invent additional substate names for it.

| State | Meaning | Canonical entry triggers | Canonical exits |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `OFF` (`414b`) | Power off. `TrainController.eb.activate()` is accepted while `OFF` and latches `eb.active = True` (per the EB workflow); that latch survives the `OFF → READY` transition and is cleared only by a later `TrainController.eb.release()` from `READY`. | Phase-3 power-off cleanup consuming `TrainController.pending_power_off` from a stopped powered state; `SimulationController.clear_failed_runtime()` (step `7c41`); the ghosted-train teardown (**Power**, Ghosted trains); startup default. Every entry except the startup default reaches `OFF` through `TrainController.complete_power_off()`. | `TrainController.commands.power_on_cmd()`, `set_mode(...)`, or `set_cab(...)` while in manual mode (any of these power `OFF → READY`; `power_on_cmd()` carries any latched `eb.active` into `READY` without clearing it) |
| `READY` (`601a`) | Powered, stopped, not dwelling. Cab may or may not be selected. | `TrainController.commands.power_on_cmd()`; `TrainController.commands.set_cab(...)` while stopped (manual mode only); the reactive stopped/no-authority rule after authority release, cancellation, or revocation; `TrainController.eb.release()` from any powered state other than `RUNNING_AT_STATION` (release from `RUNNING_AT_STATION` clears `eb.active` and stays in `RUNNING_AT_STATION`); `TrainController.finish_dwell()`; reactive speed-based evaluation inside `TrainController.on_physics_updated(span_update)` when the just-written speed satisfies `TrainController.is_stopped()` and the train has no live authority (no-authority braking completion path, both ATO and manual). A driver in manual mode who voluntarily stops under positive clearance with live authority remains in `RUNNING_MOVING` per the next row. See the **Reactive speed-based state evaluation** bullets below for the full predicate set. | `TrainController.depart()` → `RUNNING_MOVING`; reactive speed-based evaluation inside `TrainController.on_physics_updated(span_update)` when the just-written speed satisfies `not TrainController.is_stopped()` (see below); `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()`; `TrainController.commands.power_off_cmd()` → writes `pending_power_off` (no synchronous exit); the Phase-3 power-off cleanup consumer performs the `READY → OFF` transition |
| `RUNNING_MOVING` (`185a`) | Depart accepted for the current authority. The train may be physically moving, or stopped at zero ATC clearance under the same live authority while waiting for clearance / authority extension. `RUNNING_MOVING` is the canonical "depart-accepted" indicator used by stop-completion eligibility and other readers. | `TrainController.depart()` acceptance; reactive speed-based evaluation inside `TrainController.on_physics_updated(span_update)` when the controller observes `not TrainController.is_stopped()` while in `READY` (rare manual edge case: a driver pre-rolls before depart was issued; once depart is accepted the controller transitions). | `READY` on post-path completion or no-authority stop; reactive speed-based evaluation to `READY` inside `TrainController.on_physics_updated(span_update)` when a train reaches the canonical stopped predicate without live authority and `eb.active == False` (while EB is latched the controller stays here and only `eb.release()` drives `READY`); stopped-only operator commands accepted while `is_stopped()` is true: `set_cab(...)` (manual mode only) → `READY`, `set_mode(...)` → `READY`, `power_off_cmd()` → writes `pending_power_off`, with Phase-3 power-off cleanup exiting through `READY` to `OFF`. There is no direct `RUNNING_MOVING → RUNNING_AT_STATION` edge: confirmed arrival first lands in `READY`, and a later Phase-1 `enter_station_dwell()` call performs `READY → RUNNING_AT_STATION`. |
| `RUNNING_AT_STATION` (`90c4`) | Route-managed dwell at a confirmed stop task | `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` dwell-entry step calls `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` when the next non-finished task on the directed walk has `task_kind == "dwell"`. The controller does not detect dwell entry on its own; it never reads `RouteRun`. | `TrainController.finish_dwell()` (called by `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` dwell-complete step) → `READY`. EB during dwell does NOT exit `RUNNING_AT_STATION`: `TrainController.eb.activate()` is accepted and sets `eb.active = True`, but state remains `RUNNING_AT_STATION` and the dwell timer keeps running. `TrainController.eb.release()` from this state clears `eb.active = False` and stays in `RUNNING_AT_STATION`. `TrainController.commands.set_cab(...)` (manual mode only) and `TrainController.commands.set_mode(...)` from `RUNNING_AT_STATION` apply their side effects but state stays `RUNNING_AT_STATION` (their `ato.running` clear is a no-op during dwell, where `ato.running` is already `False` per **`ato.running` meaning and lifecycle**). Any of the three Phase-3 teardown consumers (`pending_route_abort`, `pending_power_off`, `pending_route_deactivate`) calls `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(...)`, which calls `finish_dwell()` to exit dwell before its own teardown continues (OFF cleanup for `pending_power_off`; run removal for the other two). |

- **Reactive speed-based state evaluation**: `TrainController.on_physics_updated(span_update)` evaluates state-machine transitions synchronously after writing the new `current_speed_kmh` and before building the `ControllerProgressReport`. The transition fires in the same tick that physics produces the speed change, so callers do not have to wait one tick to observe it. All speed comparisons here key on the canonical `TrainController.is_stopped()` predicate (per the **Units contract**), never on raw `current_speed_kmh` arithmetic. The rules are:
  - If `state == READY` and `not TrainController.is_stopped()`: transition to `RUNNING_MOVING`. This is one of the two canonical state-machine transitions into `RUNNING_MOVING` (the other is a successful `TrainController.depart()` call); both are equally legitimate first-class transitions. After this transition, if `control_mode == "ato"` and `received_path_plan is not None`, call `TrainController.ato.go()` — the canonical helper that sets `ato.running = True` per the **`ato.running` meaning and lifecycle** section. Both the `depart()` path and this reactive path route through it, so neither duplicates the precondition check. This rule covers several cases: a manual driver pre-rolls under live authority before Phase 1's `depart()` lands; ATC clearance returns under live authority and physics resumes motion before the next Phase 1 tick; and any other path that legitimately produces motion from `READY`.
  - If `state == RUNNING_MOVING` and `TrainController.is_stopped()` and the train has no live authority and `TrainController.eb.active == False`: transition to `READY`. This is the no-authority braking completion path. While live authority still exists and the train is stopped, the controller stays in `RUNNING_MOVING`; clearance may return and movement may resume without a fresh `depart()` call. While EB is latched (`eb.active == True`) the controller also stays in `RUNNING_MOVING` / `RUNNING_AT_STATION`; only `TrainController.eb.release()` drives the return to `READY` (per the EB workflow), so the reactive path must not fire while EB is active.
  - While live authority still exists, `is_stopped() == True` alone never exits `RUNNING_MOVING` — whether the stop is voluntary (the driver stopped under positive clearance) or ATC-forced (zero clearance, e.g. a best-effort truncated authority whose `authority_end_block_id` is short of the armed stop target after `extend_path(...)` appended nothing; see tick step `90d3`). In both cases clearance may return when the path extends or conditions change, and motion resumes without a fresh `depart()` call. The only stopped exit to `READY` is the no-authority case in the bullet above.
- The state machine above is the single canonical operational-state contract. Shared logic must not introduce additional live route/runtime state enums for path completion or cancellation recovery in v1.
- **`RUNNING_AT_STATION` invariant**: `received_path_plan is None` whenever `state == RUNNING_AT_STATION`. Authority release (`PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(...)`) transitions the `Path` to `COMPLETED` in Phase 3; only after that transition does Phase 1's dwell-entry step (`3a37`) fire and call `enter_station_dwell()`. No code path may call `enter_station_dwell()` while a `Path` with `status == ACTIVE` still exists for this train. This invariant is the structural basis for the EB-during-dwell skip of `pending_path_cancel_request` (row `0a8e` of the EB workflow table): because no live path exists during dwell, no path cancellation latch needs to be written.
- Stopped-only operator commands (`power_off_cmd()`, `set_cab(...)` in manual mode, and `set_mode(...)`) may be issued from any physically stopped controller state, including `OFF`. `set_mode(...)` (any mode), and `set_cab(...)` (manual mode only), issued while `OFF` also power the controller up as a side effect: they land it in `READY` (the same `OFF → READY` transition `power_on_cmd()` performs; see the `OFF` state-table row) while also applying the cab/mode change, because selecting a cab or mode is a way of bringing a stopped train under control. `power_off_cmd()` while `OFF` is accepted as a no-op (the train is already off). In v1 these commands disengage any active ATO run and land in `READY` (or `OFF` after pending power-off cleanup). They do not cause automatic movement just because clearance later returns or dwell later finishes.
- Stopped recovery commands transition to `READY` (regardless of cab); powered off remains `OFF`.

**`ato.running` meaning and lifecycle**

- `ato.running` means "an ATO run is currently in progress": the train has been commanded to move under ATO via `TrainController.depart()` / `TrainController.ato.go()`, and that run has not yet ended through arrival-at-dwell, path completion, authority clear, EB, or any stopped-state command (power / cab / mode). It is a **run-active** flag, not a **mode** flag. The mode flag is `control_mode` (`ato` vs `manual`), which says what kind of driving the controller is set up for. `ato.running` says whether an ATO run is currently driving the train. A train in ATO mode is not always `ato.running`: before the first `depart()`, between runs, during dwell, and after any run-ending event, `control_mode == ato` but `ato.running == False`. When the train is stopped with zero ATC clearance but the same live authority still exists (that is, still in `RUNNING_MOVING` with `is_stopped() == True`), the run has not ended; `ato.running` stays `True` so the controller auto-resumes when clearance returns. A train in manual mode is never `ato.running`.
- `ato.running = True` is set by the canonical state-machine transition into `RUNNING_MOVING`, whenever `control_mode == "ato"` and `received_path_plan is not None` at the moment of transition. This applies to both transition triggers: a successful `TrainController.depart()` acceptance (which calls `TrainController.ato.go()`), AND the reactive speed-based evaluation entering `RUNNING_MOVING` from `READY`. Both transitions converge on the same write. `ato.go()` is the canonical helper that performs the write, so callers do not duplicate the precondition check. No other command, tick handler, or callback flips this flag to `True`. In particular, `TrainController.commands.set_cab(...)`, `TrainController.commands.set_mode(...)`, `TrainController.eb.release()`, `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()`, and `TrainController.finish_dwell()` never set `ato.running = True`.
- `ato.running` has exactly one writer: `TrainController`. Neither `RouteExecutor` nor `PathDispatcher` nor any train-side mirror writes this flag directly; train-running state is not a route executor's concern. `controller.ato.stop()` is the canonical helper that clears `ato.running`; it is idempotent, and every canonical run-ending event calls it rather than assigning the field: successful start of dwell (`enter_station_dwell()`), authority clear, `begin_service_stop()`, EB activation, `power_off_cmd()`, `set_cab(...)`, and `set_mode(...)`. Resuming ATO after any of these events requires another `depart()` / `ato.go()` acceptance.
- A zero-ATC-clearance hold under live authority is not a run-ending event, so `ato.running` stays `True` and the controller auto-resumes when clearance returns (it is still `RUNNING_MOVING`, per that state's definition). Only the run-ending events listed above clear the flag.
- Manual mode never sets `ato.running`. In manual, `TrainController.depart()` marks the current authority ready to go and does not call `ato.go()`; `ato.running` stays `False` throughout manual driving.

**Power/cab/mode command contracts**

- `TrainController.commands.power_on_cmd()` is the dedicated `OFF -> powered` transition (`set_mode(...)` always, and `set_cab(...)` in manual mode, also power `OFF → READY` as a side effect — see their command contracts below). It is valid only when power is currently off, and it is processed in the train's Phase-2 operator-command slot. When a train starts its transaction `OFF`, `SimulationController` skips that train's route Phase 1, because planning cannot run before command drain. The Phase-2 command slot still runs, so power-on can be accepted. If `power_on_cmd()` succeeds, the controller is no longer `OFF`, so the transaction continues through the rest of the controller logic, `TrainPhysics.tick(...)`, and the normal Phase 3 feedback/cleanup pass; only fresh route planning waits for a later Phase 1 tick. The controller transitions to `READY` no matter the cab selection. `power_on_cmd()` does not touch `eb.active`: if EB was latched while the train was `OFF`, the controller lands in `READY` with `eb.active == True` (held at a stand by EB), and the operator clears it with the ordinary `TrainController.eb.release()` from `READY`.
- `TrainController.commands.power_off_cmd()` is valid only while stopped, including with EB latched. `_apply_power_off_request()` performs the exact controller-local writes in Power-off step `98d3`: call `controller.ato.stop()`, clear the `ops.service_stop_active` latch through `consume_service_stop()`, neutralize `handle_position`, call `manual.reset_power_percent_for_safety()`, and write the power-off/inclusive-cancel latches. Pending power-off then suppresses any later propulsion command in the same Phase 2. Dispatcher/route teardown remains Phase 3; world physics continues running with the neutral command.
- `TrainController.complete_power_off()` is the controller-owned OFF-cleanup entry point and the canonical `OFF` writer. It is callable whenever the controller is powered; it does not require `pending_power_off` to be set. It transitions `state` to `OFF`, clears the cab through `controller.commands.clear_cab()` and the EB latch through `controller.eb.clear()`, calls `controller.ato.stop()`, clears the `ops.service_stop_active` latch through `consume_service_stop()`, neutralizes `handle_position` through `ops.reset_handle_for_safety()`, sets `physics_sync.current_speed_kmh` to `0`, and consumes `pending_power_off` when that latch is set — consuming it is a no-op when it is not. Three paths call it, and none of them assigns any of those fields itself: the Phase-3 power-off consumer (step `886a`), `SimulationController.clear_failed_runtime()` (step `7c41`), and the ghosted-train teardown (**Power**, Ghosted trains). Startup is the only other `OFF` source. Routing all three through this one method is what keeps the `OFF` row of **Allowed `(state, cab)` pairs** true, and keeps `eb.active` assigned only by the three EB methods.
- `TrainController.commands.set_cab(cab)` is an operator manual-driving command. It is valid only while `control_mode == "manual"` and the train is stopped (`is_stopped() == True`), which includes `RUNNING_AT_STATION` (dwell is a stopped state). In ATO, the controller aligns the cab itself during `TrainController.depart()` (see the **`TrainController.depart()` canonical contract**), so an operator-issued `set_cab` in ATO mode is rejected with a diagnostic such as `"cab selection is only available in manual mode"`. That ATO auto-selection is the system path, separate from this operator command, and is not gated by this rule. If the requested cab equals the current cab (including a same-letter no-op or `None == None`), `set_cab(...)` is a no-op success: it does NOT change controller state, and it emits no event beyond the ordinary command-result acknowledgement. Otherwise it does not create motion or clear any assigned dispatcher authority. Whenever the accepted selection moves the head to the other physical end — `A <-> B`, and equally `None -> B` and `B -> None`, because the head defaults to `end_a` with no cab — it re-expresses the span's two end frames in the reversed travel direction, per the **TrainSpan schema**. Because the command is manual-only, there is no active ATO run for an operator `set_cab` to disengage (`ato.running` is always `False` in manual mode per the **`ato.running` meaning and lifecycle**), and changing cab invalidates any prior manual depart-acceptance. State-transition rule: when called from `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, `set_cab(None)` is rejected (`success=False` with a diagnostic such as `"cannot deselect cab while dwelling"`), because `(RUNNING_AT_STATION, None)` is not a legal `(state, cab)` pair per the **Allowed `(state, cab)` pairs** table; setting a different non-`None` cab letter is accepted and the state stays `RUNNING_AT_STATION` (dwell is preserved; the side effects above still apply). When called from `READY`, the state stays `READY` (only `cab` changes). When called from `OFF` (the controller already being in manual mode), the command powers the controller up to `READY` and applies the cab (selecting a cab brings a stopped train under control). When called from `RUNNING_MOVING`, `set_cab(...)` is rejected (`success=False`, for example `"Cannot switch cab while moving"`) only while the train is actually moving (`is_stopped() == False`); when the train is held stopped at the live-authority boundary (`RUNNING_MOVING` with `is_stopped() == True`, for example a zero-clearance hold), the `is_stopped()` precondition is satisfied, and `set_cab(...)` is accepted and transitions the controller to `READY` per the **Controller state machine** table. If the selected cab points away from the current live authority, the controller must not rewrite that authority. Instead it rebuilds lookahead in the selected travel frame; if that frame has no forward authority, the distance-0 ATC limit becomes `0`, so ATC prevents wrong-direction movement under the mismatched cab choice. A fresh `TrainController.depart()` acceptance is required to re-arm departure.
- `TrainController.commands.set_mode(mode)` is valid only while the train is stopped (`is_stopped() == True`), which includes `RUNNING_AT_STATION`. It switches between `ato` and `manual`, disengages any active ATO run by calling `controller.ato.stop()`, clears the retained percentage through `manual.reset_power_percent_for_safety()`, and does not create or clear dispatcher authority. The percentage clear keeps a `power_percent` command from surviving a mode change and driving the train when manual `power_percent` is selected again, and it keeps the **driver-control interlock** on `TrainController.eb.release()` reachable: a driver who leaves `power_percent` submode can no longer be holding a percentage they have no accepted command to clear. `manual` is a control mode, not a separate operational state. State-transition rule: when called from `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, the state stays `RUNNING_AT_STATION` (dwell is preserved). When called from `READY`, the state stays `READY`. When called from `RUNNING_MOVING` (possible only while `is_stopped() == True`, since the command is rejected while the train is moving), the state transitions to `READY` per the **Controller state machine** table. When called from `OFF`, the command powers the controller up to `READY`.
- `TrainController.commands.set_manual_mode_type(mode_type)` is the canonical setter for `manual.manual_mode_type`. It is valid only while the train is stopped (`is_stopped() == True`, which includes `RUNNING_AT_STATION` — dwell is a stopped state), powered, and `control_mode == manual`. It never changes `state`: called from `RUNNING_AT_STATION` the state stays `RUNNING_AT_STATION` (dwell is preserved), and called from `READY` it stays `READY`. Side effects: when entering `power_percent`, reset `handle_position` to neutral; when entering `simulation`, clear the retained percentage through the Manual-driving sub-module's reset helper. It preserves cab selection and `received_path_plan`, and does not start movement. The construction default for `manual.manual_mode_type` is `simulation`. The frontend/API and editor/UI must expose this setter through the same operator-input route as `set_handle()` / `set_manual_power_percent()`.
- `TrainController.commands.set_handle()` accepts only when `control_mode == manual`, `manual_mode_type == simulation`, `state != "OFF"`, and a cab is selected; otherwise it returns `success=False`. `TrainController.commands.set_manual_power_percent(percent)` accepts under those same driver-input conditions except `manual.manual_mode_type` must be `power_percent`. It clamps `percent` to `0..100`, calls `manual.set_power_percent(percent)`, preserves `received_path_plan`, and does not start movement by itself. Both commands are still accepted while `eb.active == True`: the operator may move the handle or set any power level, but EB overrides motion — the EB Workflow holds `handle_position` at `EB` for as long as the latch is set, and the zero ATC limit at distance 0 keeps a retained percentage from producing movement. Neither command clears `eb.active`, which only `TrainController.eb.release()` releases, and in `power_percent` submode that release is gated on `manual.manual_power_percent == 0`, so `set_manual_power_percent(0)` is how the driver clears the control before releasing. `TrainController.commands.set_handle()` is rejected (`success=False`) when `manual_mode_type == power_percent`. `TrainController.commands.set_manual_power_percent()` is rejected (`success=False`) when `manual_mode_type == simulation` or when any of the shared acceptance preconditions fail.
- There is no separate `TrainController.set_travel_direction(...)` command in v1. Manual direction selection is only by cab selection through `TrainController.commands.set_cab(...)`.
- `TrainController.eb.activate()` is the canonical operator-issued EB command, and on purpose it has no other precondition: emergency means it must be available at all times. Its only acceptance gate is `eb.active == False` (a duplicate activation while EB is already latched is rejected as a no-op). It is valid in any motion state, any operational state including `OFF`, with or without a cab, and with or without a live path. On acceptance it performs the **canonical EB activation effects** defined in the **EB workflow state machine**. Exception while `state == RUNNING_AT_STATION`: the controller is dwelling and has no live path (`received_path_plan is None`), so no `pending_path_cancel_request` is written (per row `0a8e` of the EB workflow table). State stays `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, and the dwell timer keeps running, undisturbed.
- `TrainController.eb.release()` accepts when `state != "OFF"`, `eb.active == True`, `TrainController.is_stopped() == True`, the controller's own `pending_path_cancel_request` is `None` (the EB cancel latch has been consumed), the controller's own `received_path_plan` is `None` (the pre-EB authority has transitioned out of `ACTIVE` in `PathDispatcher`), and the **driver-control interlock** below is satisfied. Otherwise it returns `success=False` and leaves controller state unchanged. The latch conditions prevent same-command-drain EB activation/release from setting recovery latches before Phase 3 has committed the EB cancellation. The check uses only controller-owned fields; no dispatcher read is needed.
- **Driver-control interlock.** When `control_mode == "manual"` and `manual.manual_mode_type == "power_percent"`, `TrainController.eb.release()` additionally requires `manual.manual_power_percent == 0`, and is otherwise rejected with a diagnostic such as `"return power to zero before releasing the emergency brake"`. The driver satisfies it with `TrainController.commands.set_manual_power_percent(0)`, which is accepted while `eb.active == True` per the **Power/cab/mode command contracts**. This condition exists because the two manual driver inputs are owned differently while EB is latched: the EB Workflow holds `handle_position` at `EB` for as long as the latch is set, so the handle is already at a braking position when the latch clears, while `manual.manual_power_percent` has no such owner and is set-and-forget per tick step `924d`. Without the interlock a percentage retained from before or during the EB stop would become the train's command the moment EB cleared, with no fresh driver input. The condition is scoped to the `power_percent` submode because `set_manual_power_percent(...)` is rejected in every other submode, so a wider gate could not be satisfied by any operator command. On acceptance from `state == RUNNING_AT_STATION` (EB had been raised during dwell, no live path was ever cancelled), state stays `RUNNING_AT_STATION` and dwell timing is unaffected. On acceptance from any other powered state, follow the standard release behavior described in the EB workflow state machine.

**EB workflow state machine**

This is the single canonical step-by-step contract for EB activation, release, and post-EB restart in v1. Shared contracts must not invent separate EB-recovery state flags on `TrainController` or `RouteExecutor` beyond the ones listed below.

**Canonical EB activation effects** (referenced elsewhere by name): Sets `eb.active = True`, forces handle to `EB`, calls `controller.ato.stop()`, clears the `ops.service_stop_active` latch through `TrainController.consume_service_stop()`, and records `pending_path_cancel_request` with `error.origin = "eb"` if a live path exists; see the Latch Table for multi-writer overwrite behavior. Other sites must cross-reference this paragraph by name and must not restate the effect list.

| Step | Trigger | Actor | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 (`4c64`). EB activation | ATC overspeed escalation (including the zero-limit branch in step `826a`, where the canonical lookahead reports `atc_limit = 0` with `atc_limit_reason_code = "deviation"` under a pending deviation, or `"no_authority"` with no plan, while the train is still rolling), which calls `TrainController.eb.activate()`, or the operator-issued `TrainController.eb.activate()` command | `TrainController.tick()` (Phase 2) for all EB paths: operator-issued EB is accepted during the tick-entry command drain, while ATC / zero-limit EB is raised later in the same tick call | Both triggers run the one activation routine `TrainController.eb.activate()`, which performs the **canonical EB activation effects** above; the controller then enters the early-exit tick loop. The same train's later Phase 3 consume pass commits the pending cancellation through `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)` once the train has stopped (per the gating in the Latch Table); if no active path exists when the gate opens (the latch was written with no live path), the dispatcher cancel mutation is skipped per the **Phase-3 latched-cancel consumption: stopped-train gating and no-active-path skip** rule and the latch is still consumed (controller-local EB state stays owned by the EB workflow). Ordinary tick work is skipped until the train stops and EB is released. |
| 1a (`0a8e`). EB activation while dwelling | `TrainController.eb.activate()` is accepted while `state == RUNNING_AT_STATION` | `TrainController.tick()` (Phase 2) | Sets `eb.active = True`, forces handle to `EB`, and does NOT write `pending_path_cancel_request` (no live path during dwell). State stays `RUNNING_AT_STATION`. Dwell timer (`task_runs[walk_position].started_at` with `task_kind == "dwell"`) is unaffected; dwell continues to count toward `RouteTask.should_depart(...)`. The timer is unaffected because `started_at` is the derived read `task_runs[walk_position].started_at` (per the **RouteTaskRun schema**) and the EB activation effect list does not touch it. Dwell *completion* is suppressed while `eb.active == True`: the Phase 1 dwell-complete step must not call `TrainController.finish_dwell()` or depart the train while EB is latched, so even once `should_depart(...)` is satisfied the train stays `RUNNING_AT_STATION` until `TrainController.eb.release()` (row `1bbc`) clears `eb.active`. No deviation/cancellation effects fire. |
| 2 (`e67d`). Rolling stop under EB | Physics integrates EB deceleration | `TrainPhysics.tick()` | Train decelerates according to `SpeedProfile.emergency_brake_rate`; `current_span` and `current_speed_kmh` continue to update normally. No additional authority completion/cancellation side effects fire during rolling beyond the earlier EB-cancellation request, but physics remains authoritative: `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` continues emitting span updates, and the same train's Phase 3 consume pass continues committing physical entry and tail-vacate resource updates while EB is latched. |
| 3 (`9f74`). Full stop under EB | `TrainController.is_stopped()` returns `True` | `TrainController.tick()` | Controller remains in the EB early-exit loop with `eb.active == True`; no state transition yet. Dispatcher path completion is explicitly suppressed while `eb.active == True`. |
| 4 (`ad04`). EB release | `TrainController.eb.release()` called while stopped and powered after the EB cancellation latch has been consumed (controller's own `pending_path_cancel_request` is `None`), dispatcher has transitioned the pre-EB active path out of `ACTIVE` (controller's own `received_path_plan is None`), and the **driver-control interlock** in the `TrainController.eb.release()` contract is satisfied | operator | Clears `eb.active = False`; transitions controller to `READY`. If the EB cancellation is still pending, the old live path still exists, or a manual `power_percent` driver still has a non-zero `manual.manual_power_percent`, the release command fails through `NotificationCenter` and does not transition state. `TrainController.eb.release()` does not call `RouteExecutor`, does not inspect or complete the cancelled pre-EB path, and does not clear route-owned recovery state itself. |
| 4a (`1bbc`). EB release from dwell | `TrainController.eb.release()` called while `state == RUNNING_AT_STATION`. The latch part of the acceptance gate (`pending_path_cancel_request is None`, `received_path_plan is None`, `is_stopped() == True`) is naturally satisfied because no live path was cancelled by the dwell EB and the dwelling controller has speed `0`. The **driver-control interlock** is not automatically satisfied: a manual `power_percent` driver dwelling with a retained percentage must still zero it first, because clearing `eb.active` here lifts dwell-completion suppression and the retained percentage would drive the train on departure. | operator | Clears `eb.active = False`; state stays `RUNNING_AT_STATION`; dwell timer is unaffected. Clearing `eb.active` lifts the dwell-completion suppression described in row `0a8e`, so the next Phase 1 dwell-complete step may finish the dwell and depart once `should_depart(...)` is satisfied. No EB-specific restart/replan is needed for this branch — row `29a0` does not apply to the dwell EB cycle. |
| 5 (`29a0`). EB restart/replan | `RouteRun.path_cancelled == True` (set by the EB cancellation callback) AND `controller.is_recovery_clean` is true on the next eligible `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` | `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` (Phase 1) | The Phase 1 route-deviation-cleanup step clears `RouteRun.path_cancelled` when a route run exists, so the derived diagnostic status returns to `RUNNING`. The same Phase 1 then continues into the ordinary planning kernel: covered-task index advance → `_find_next_task_target(train_id)` → fresh `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` → `TrainController.depart()` per the canonical depart contract. |

- `eb.active` is controller-owned, but EB activation must request dispatcher cancellation through `pending_path_cancel_request` immediately when a live path exists. The actual cancellation side effects still run only inside `PathDispatcher` during the same train's later Phase 3 consume pass; `TrainController` never releases resources itself.
- EB recovery uses the same recovery latches as any other cancellation: `RouteRun.path_cancelled` for route-owned recovery cleanup. No EB-specific route hook or auto-depart flag exists in v1.
- `eb.active` while `OFF`: `TrainController.eb.activate()` is accepted in `OFF` and latches `eb.active = True` with no live path to cancel. The latch survives `OFF → READY`: `power_on_cmd()` does not clear it, so a train powered on with EB latched rests in `(state == READY, eb.active == True)`, held at a stand by EB, until the operator issues the ordinary `TrainController.eb.release()` from `READY` (row `ad04`). There is no separate OFF-state EB clear path; `eb.release()` is rejected while `OFF` per its `state != "OFF"` gate.

**received_path_plan schema**

- This is the single canonical schema for the controller's active path-plan payload. Every producer/consumer must refer back to this subsection by name instead of restating the field list elsewhere.
- **Why this shape.** The controller's job is to brake to the live authority boundary using its safety margin and to compute speed restrictions live from the network and the authorized corridor. That work needs the corridor geometry (`resource_sequence`) and the boundary (`authority_end_block_id`); it does not need the dispatcher-side requested-target fields, which are a `PathDispatcher` completion-enforcement concern. Keeping the projection narrow lets the controller stay decoupled from dispatcher-side completion logic and lets `received_path_plan` regenerate as a pure read with no caching.
- `received_path_plan` carries exactly `path_id`, `resource_sequence`, and `authority_end_block_id` in v1. It is the controller's only active path-plan payload. `resource_sequence` uses the canonical **resource_sequence entry schema**, and each nested `path_tiles` slice uses the canonical **path_tile entry schema**, so the controller can extend lookahead along the actual geometry without inferring terminal-frame direction from tile coordinates alone. The requested target (`requested_destination_block_id`, `requested_destination_direction`) is stored on the dispatcher-owned `Path` record but is not included in `received_path_plan`; the controller does not see those fields. Arrival enforcement against the requested target belongs to the **Stop-arrival check**, which reads route-owned state.
- `authority_end_block_id` in `received_path_plan` is always the current authority boundary the controller brakes toward, whether or not that authority has reached the run's armed target. The controller does not know the armed target and does not need to: `Path.reaches_target(block_id)` answers that question for the route side.
- `received_path_plan` does not carry a separate cab or head-side field. The ordered `resource_sequence` is the combined already-occupied physical span plus forward path authority in tail-to-authority order. "Tail" means the physical end of the current span farther from `authority_end_block_id`; "authority side" means the physical end closer to the destination and pointing into the path authority. When `TrainController.depart()` runs in ATO with no cab selected, it derives the cab by comparing `TrainSpan.end_a` and `TrainSpan.end_b` against that ordered seeded span and chooses the cab whose physical end is on the authority side, per the **`TrainController.depart()` canonical contract** (manual depart never auto-selects — it requires a pre-selected cab and rejects when `cab is None`). `end_a` is not a general preferred end in this model; it is only the final dead-heat tie-break when every authority-side comparison between the two physical ends remains exactly tied.
- Treat `assigned_path_id`, `ato_path_id`, and `ato_destination_block_id` as computed reads derived from `received_path_plan`, not as independently written runtime fields. The alias mapping is explicit in v1:
  - `assigned_path_id := received_path_plan.path_id`
  - `ato_path_id := received_path_plan.path_id`
  - `ato_destination_block_id := received_path_plan.authority_end_block_id`
  When `PathDispatcher` clears authority by transitioning `Path.status` to `COMPLETED`/`CANCELLED`, the controller's `received_path_plan` property returns `None` on its next read and all three disappear automatically.
- `lookahead` is a canonical pure derived value computed live on every read; it is the single canonical series used by ATC, ATO, UI, and monitor consumers.
- Target speed and ATC limit are reads from lookahead at distance 0; the controller does not store standalone `target_speed` or `effective_limit` scalars. The exported v1 payload also carries a top-level `speed_limit_reason` field; its canonical source is the distance-0 lookahead speed-curve metadata, surfaced at top level for UI/monitor reads.

**canonical lookahead payload**

- `TrainController.get_lookahead()` is the single canonical exported lookahead payload for ATC, ATO, UI, and monitor consumers. Every producer/consumer must refer back to this subsection by name instead of restating the field list elsewhere.
- **Why this shape.** Lookahead is the single read surface that fuses authority geometry, network state, signals, and current motion into one ordered forward projection. ATC, ATO, monitor UI, and stop-completion all sample from the same payload, so there is one place to add or change a forward-projected datapoint. Speed-curve metadata (including `atc_limit_reason_code`) lives on the same payload so that "why is the train holding at zero?" is answerable from one read instead of three. The empty/no-cab/no-train form preserves the same shape so consumers do not need to branch on payload variant.
- In v1, `get_lookahead()` carries exactly:
  - top level: `train_id`, `head_tile`, `head_distance_cm`, `travel_direction`, `cab`, `speed_limit_reason`, `lookahead`
  - nested `lookahead`: `blocks`, `signals`, `switches`, `stations`, `clearance_cm`, `safety_margin_cm`, `target_stop_cm`, `authority_boundary_delta_cm`, `speed_curves`
- `head_tile` is the current train head tile coordinate or `None` when unavailable. `head_distance_cm`, `clearance_cm`, `safety_margin_cm`, `target_stop_cm`, and `authority_boundary_delta_cm` are centimeters. `target_stop_cm` is the braking-target distance from the head (`clearance_cm` minus the effective `safety_margin_cm`, floored at 0; equal to `authority_boundary_delta_cm`). `travel_direction` is the train's current forward travel direction in the same frame used by movement/traversal. `cab` is the currently selected cab or `None`.
- `blocks`, `signals`, `switches`, and `stations` are ordered forward projections for UI/debug consumers. They are informational read models only; control decisions come from the same payload's clearance and speed-curve data.
- `lookahead.stations` is the payload name for the ordered forward platform projection: each entry refers to a concrete physical platform ahead on the track, not to a logical multi-platform station group.
- Each `lookahead.blocks` entry carries exactly:
  - `id`
  - `name`
  - `state`
  - `start_cm`
  - `end_cm`
  - `length_cm`
  - `tile_count`
  - `speed_limit_kmh`
- Each `lookahead.signals` entry carries exactly:
  - `id`
  - `name`
  - `state`
  - `distance_cm`
- Each `lookahead.switches` entry carries exactly:
  - `x`
  - `y`
  - `name`
  - `position`
  - `distance_cm`
  - `locked`
  where `position` is `"main"` or `"diverging"` for the live switch alignment at that tile, and `locked` is the exported boolean monitor view of whether that switch currently has any future lock claim or train-owned protection claim that blocks conflicting acquisition.
- Each `lookahead.stations` entry carries exactly:
  - `platform_id`
  - `station_id`
  - `name`
  - `distance_cm`
  - `length_cm`
  - `tile_count`
  - `top_speed_kmh`
- `platform_id` in this compatibility payload is the concrete physical platform identifier on the track.
- `station_id` in this compatibility payload is the logical station-group identifier that owns that platform, or `None` when no station group exists. `station_id` must not be reused as the physical platform identifier anywhere.
- Each `lookahead.speed_curves` entry carries exactly:
  - `distance_cm`
  - `atc_limit`
  - `operational_limit`
  - optional `atc_limit_reason_code`
  - optional `atc_limit_reason_detail`
  The limit fields are in `km/h`.
- The canonical `atc_limit_reason_code` enumeration is defined here once. Every consumer (the **Stop-arrival check**, no-authority braking diagnostic, and monitor/UI display) cross-references this set by name and must not use arbitrary strings or redefine the values elsewhere. `TrainController.is_stopped()` is speed-only and does not consume a reason code.
  - `"authority_stop"` (`f169`) — zero-speed restriction at the safety-margin-adjusted end of live authority, including a head that has rolled past that boundary while still mapping into `resource_sequence`; the sole code that satisfies the **Stop-arrival check**
  - `"caution"` (`39ed`) — approaching or inside a caution zone (block in `CAUTION` state or forward block occupied)
  - `"obstruction"` (`05e9`) — an in-path resource conflict that blocks forward progress below safe clearance distance
  - `"speed_limit"` (`5c1d`) — a posted segment speed limit (`Segment.speed_limit_kmh`) below the train's current maximum: a permitted-speed restriction ahead, distinct from `obstruction` (which is an in-path resource conflict, not a posted limit)
  - `"no_authority"` (`7f1a`) — no live path authority; `atc_limit = 0` at distance 0 regardless of block geometry, so ATC holds a stopped train at rest and escalates a rolling train to EB
  - `"deviation"` (`4e2b`) — live authority exists but the train head cannot be mapped into `received_path_plan.resource_sequence`; `atc_limit = 0` at distance 0 so ATC stops the train (escalating a rolling train to EB per step `826a`) while the latched deviation cancel waits for the stop
  - `"no_cab"` (`2ce5`) — no cab selected; ATC limit is zero regardless of authority
  - `"wrong_direction_cab"` (`a54f`) — selected cab points away from live authority direction; ATC limit is zero
  - `"eb"` (`c3ea`) — emergency brake latched; ATC limit is zero
  - `"no_train"` (`7b09`) — the train or network object is not yet wired, so the empty distance-0 anchor still reports a structured reason code rather than a free-form string
  - **Tie-break.** When more than one category above produces the distance-0 minimum, the reported code is the first match in this order: `no_train`, `no_cab`, `eb`, `no_authority`, `deviation`, `wrong_direction_cab`, `obstruction`, `authority_stop`, `caution`, `speed_limit`. `authority_stop` therefore never masks a zero-limit condition that is not an arrival, keeping the **Stop-arrival check**'s `atc_limit_reason_code == "authority_stop"` conjunct exact.
- **A changed reason code is an event.** The reason code answers "why is this train limited right now", so the runtime records when that answer changes. Tick substep `4d7c` compares this tick's distance-0 `atc_limit_reason_code` against the stored `atc.last_emitted_reason_code` and, when they differ, emits one event through `controller.notifications` per the **Event schema**: `operation = "tick"`, `entities` naming the train, `message` one line naming the previous and new code, and `payload` carrying the previous code, the new code, the new `atc_limit`, and the `atc_limit_reason_detail`. It then writes the new code to `atc.last_emitted_reason_code`. The substep declares `kind` itself, the same way every other writer does: `"info"` for `"deviation"` and `"no_authority"`, because those hold the train at zero and an operator wants them in the stream, and `"debug"` for every other code, because they are ordinary driving restrictions a developer reads back. The level is written into the emit call, never computed from the code value at run time. The event carries no `ErrorRecord`: it reports what the limit is, not that something failed, and the failure that caused the restriction is reported by its own emitting operation. Keeping it below `"warning"` also keeps it out of `NotificationCenter.latest_error_for(...)`, so the no-authority banner still answers with a real failure. The comparison is against the last emitted code, not the last tick's code, so a code that flickers back and forth emits on each real change and a steady code emits nothing — the same **Emit on change, not per tick** rule the **Error events** section states. The payload is the whole record: nothing else stores a reason-code history, and the live value stays the lookahead read it always was.
- `atc_limit_reason_code == "authority_stop"` is the canonical reason code for the zero-speed restriction at the active stop/end-of-authority boundary. The **Stop-arrival check** must key on this code, not on arbitrary reason strings.
- `authority_boundary_delta_cm` is the live signed separation from the train head to the safety-margin-adjusted authority boundary in the same forward distance axis as the `speed_curves[*].distance_cm` samples and the authority-boundary projection helper. With a live path it is derived from `authority_end_block_id` plus the terminal frame; without a live path it is `0.0` because no authority boundary exists. It is diagnostic/control lookahead data, not a route-authored stop-position field.
- Consumers asking "what is the target speed right now?" sample `lookahead.speed_curves` at distance `0`: `operational_limit` at distance `0` is the controller target speed, and `atc_limit` plus its reason metadata at distance `0` is the controller ATC limit/reason. The exported payload carries the top-level `speed_limit_reason` field — the same distance-0 reason metadata surfaced at top level for monitor/UI reads.
- The empty/no-cab/no-train form must preserve this same top-level and nested key shape, with empty lists and zero distances instead of returning a different payload shape. If a compatibility producer cannot compute one of the nested boundary/safety fields on an empty payload, it must still emit the key with `0.0` rather than omitting it.

**Controller tick order**

1. `01f2` — Drain queued operator commands as the first substep of `TrainController.tick(...)`.
1a. `4d7c` — **Reason-code change event.** Read the distance-0 `atc_limit_reason_code` from `lookahead` and, when it differs from `atc.last_emitted_reason_code`, emit one event and store the new code per **A changed reason code is an event**. This substep runs ahead of the `3ec8` and `d483` early exits, so the `"no_train"`, `"no_cab"`, and `"eb"` codes emit like every other code.
2. `3ec8` — If no train, return immediately. The drain has already run, so a command queued against a train that is absent from the network fails its own precondition check rather than sitting in the queue. After that drain, if `state == "OFF"`, keep the controller in `OFF` and return from the controller tick's powered decision work. `SimulationController` still runs `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` and `TrainController.on_physics_updated(...)` for storage and physics continuity, but that callback must append no dispatcher/route progress reports to the mailbox beyond the block observations folded in from the Step-0 detected sets, and Phase 3 is skipped for that still-off transaction unless those sets carried a block entry or vacate this tick. A train that moves without power — one pushed by hand, or an unidentified train (see **Power**, Unidentified trains) — still has its block entries and vacates committed. If `TrainController.commands.power_on_cmd()` succeeded during the drain, the controller is no longer `OFF`, so `TrainController.tick(...)` continues into normal powered controller logic, `SimulationController` runs `TrainPhysics.tick(...)`, and Phase 3 runs normally for that same transaction. Route Phase 1 stays skipped only for the transaction that began while the train was `OFF`; route planning resumes on the next eligible tick.
3. `d483` — If emergency brake is active:
  - Hold the EB state: re-write handle = `EB` and force the ATC limit at distance 0 to `0`. Then early-exit the tick while EB remains latched. The other **canonical EB activation effects** are not re-applied here, because activation already called `controller.ato.stop()` and cleared the `ops.service_stop_active` latch, no path re-enters authority while EB is latched, and Phase 1 run step `6a0d` does not re-arm the service-stop latch while a higher-priority teardown latch is pending, so there is nothing left to re-apply. `manual.manual_power_percent` is the one manual input this step does not own — the driver may re-command it on any EB tick per the **Power/cab/mode command contracts** — and it needs no per-tick reset here, because the forced zero ATC limit keeps it from producing movement while the latch is set and the **driver-control interlock** on `TrainController.eb.release()` keeps a retained value from surviving into the release.
  - Stay in the early-exit loop until the train has stopped.
  - Require explicit `eb.release()` to clear `eb.active`.
  - While `eb.active == True`, `TrainController.tick()` does not perform post-EB restart logic. `TrainController.eb.release()` owns the transition to the **`READY` state** (per the predicate defined in the Controller state machine subsection); that release step does not clear route-owned recovery state. The earlier EB activation already wrote `pending_path_cancel_request` (`error.origin = "eb"`), which Phase 3 cancels and which sets `RouteRun.path_cancelled = True` via the cancellation callback. `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` (Phase 1) owns the route-deviation cleanup step and observes `path_cancelled` once the controller has reached the `READY` state.
4. `061a` — If no cab is selected, ATC limit is forced to zero and `lookahead` returns the canonical empty payload on every read.
5. `22a9` — Otherwise `lookahead` returns the canonical payload for the current state on every read:
  - With a received path plan, lookahead extends along the path to the current `authority_end_block_id` boundary adjusted by the controller safety margin.
  - With a received path plan whose head cannot be mapped into `resource_sequence` (a pending deviation), the distance-0 anchor reports `atc_limit = 0` with `atc_limit_reason_code = "deviation"`; the forward projections carry whatever geometry remains mappable.
  - Without a path plan, `lookahead` is the canonical no-authority empty payload (same top-level and nested shape as the no-cab case). `speed_curves` carries a single anchor at `distance_cm = 0.0` with `atc_limit = 0.0`, `operational_limit = 0.0`, and `atc_limit_reason_code = no_authority`. `blocks`, `signals`, `switches`, and `stations` are empty lists. `clearance_cm = 0.0` and `authority_boundary_delta_cm = 0.0`. The controller does not project forward through the train's current occupied block when no live path exists. Movement authority is binary on `received_path_plan`, per the **`received_path_plan` schema** rule that there is no second authority source.
6. `c4c2` — Build ATC state from `lookahead`, including structured reason metadata.
7. `914f` — Apply ATC overspeed logic.
8. `0a4e` — Calculate ATO info.
9. `8d18` — In ATO mode, if `ato.running`, calculate the handle with `controller.ato.compute_handle(delta_seconds)`.
10. `924d` — In manual mode, write a controller-owned command and let physics convert it to speed:
  - In `simulation` submode, store the driver's handle notch in `handle_position` and allow ATC to override with braking.
  - In `power_percent` submode, store the driver's percentage power in `manual.manual_power_percent` as an integer `0..100`, and let physics map it through `SpeedProfile.power_speed_curve` when no braking override is active. This value is positive only. It represents the absolute applied track/motor voltage and its calibrated immediate running speed, not a signed traction-vs-brake effort. `manual.manual_power_percent` is set-and-forget: once a driver `set_manual_power_percent(...)` command writes it, or a safety workflow resets it to `0` through `manual.reset_power_percent_for_safety()`, it stays at that value across ticks until the driver issues a new command. The controller stores no second driver-command copy and never restores a pre-override value automatically.
  - **Canonical power-percent safety-cap ordering.** At the start of command selection, sample `commanded_power = manual.manual_power_percent` and derive `commanded_speed = power_speed_curve(commanded_power)`. With no braking override, that operating point is the effective speed/power/voltage for the tick. With an ATC, service-stop, or EB override, compute `effective_speed = min(commanded_speed, override_cap)`, where `override_cap` is `atc_limit_at_distance_0` for an ATC override (which already reads `0` for an EB override, via `atc_limit_reason_code = "eb"`) and `0.0`, supplied directly by tick step `b41e`, for a service-stop override; then derive the tick's `effective_power` from the inverse `power_speed_curve` mapping and `track_voltage` from `voltage_power_curve`. Under an ATC or service-stop override, and only after capturing that effective operating point, call `manual.reset_power_percent_for_safety()`, so the retained field is `0` for later ticks until the driver re-commands it. An EB override caps the tick to zero but leaves the retained field alone: the driver clears it, and `TrainController.eb.release()` is gated on that clear through its **driver-control interlock**. Thus a positive ATC cap can produce a positive capped voltage for the current tick without contradicting the retained reset, while a zero cap produces zero speed/power/voltage. There is no handle fallback or negative power-percent command.
10a. `b41e` — **Route-owned service-stop override.** When `ops.service_stop_active == True`, this step runs after ordinary ATO/manual command generation and replaces that tick's effective command with service stopping. In ATO it keeps `ato.running = False` and uses the ordinary service-brake-to-zero handle. In manual `simulation` it overwrites the active handle with the service-brake-to-zero notch after any queued driver write. In manual `power_percent` it uses the canonical safety-cap ordering with a zero cap and calls `manual.reset_power_percent_for_safety()`; the direct-voltage model therefore stops immediately at its calibrated zero point. EB and ATC may still demand stronger braking and always outrank this override. The override persists across ticks until `TrainController.consume_service_stop()` clears the latch after stopped no-authority confirmation; driver commands may be acknowledged while it is active but cannot produce propulsion.
11. `44e9` — No standalone `effective_limit` or `target_speed` scalar is computed; consumers read controller lookahead at distance 0.
12. `89ba` — `TrainController.tick()` does not latch path completion. It builds the current state-machine outputs by reading `lookahead` (which recomputes live from current span/speed/path), but it must not mutate `Path` records, release resources, clear authority, or call route callbacks itself. The paired `TrainController.on_physics_updated(...)` callback only updates `current_span` / `current_speed_kmh`; subsequent `lookahead` reads automatically reflect the new values.
13. `32dd` — Let waiting decisions come from controller lookahead plus the route-owned armed target, read live from `RouteRun.task_runs[walk_position].resolved_target`. Here `walk_position` is the index returned by `_find_next_task_target(train_id)` for the live planning chain (the canonical writer of `task_runs[walk_position].resolved_target` is the Phase 1 `create_path` / `extend_path` step). Let path-completion commits come only from the later Phase 3 consume pass. During Phase 3, after earlier local progress reports have been consumed and any movement-derived deviation handling from them has run, `SimulationController` runs the **Stop-arrival check** against the controller's current state only (the predicate defined in step `8df5`). The route-owned armed target is an input to that check, because the stop being served is the train's next non-finished task. On success, `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id)` releases the authority and hands the arrival to the route, which records the finished stop (timestamps per **Time sampling**). Any explicit latched teardown such as `pending_path_cancel_request` is handled only after that normal arrival opportunity. `TrainController.tick()` itself does not commit completion.
14. `90d3` — A best-effort truncated path stopping at its current authority boundary is not completion when that boundary is short of the route-owned armed stop target. The controller stays in `RUNNING_MOVING` with `is_stopped() == True` (depart was accepted; ATC is holding the train at zero); the same `received_path_plan.path_id` remains live while `RouteExecutor` keeps retrying `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` on it.
15. `826a` — **Zero-limit authority-loss / deviation protocol.** A rolling train whose authority can no longer be used is stopped through the distance-0 zero limit, never through a service-brake-to-boundary path. Two cases share it. (1) Deviation under a still-live path: the deviation latch is pending while the path stays `ACTIVE`, and the canonical lookahead reads `atc_limit = 0` at distance 0 with `atc_limit_reason_code = "deviation"` (head unmappable, per step `22a9`). (2) No live path at all (`received_path_plan is None`): the lookahead is the no-authority empty payload (`atc_limit_reason_code = "no_authority"`). Because every cancellation commits only on a stopped train, case (2) cannot arise from a cancel under a rolling train; a stopped train whose authority was cancelled simply rests under the no-authority zero limit. In either case, any `current_speed_kmh` above zero is overspeed at distance 0, and the ATC overspeed branch in step `914f` escalates to EB by calling `controller.eb.activate()`, which performs the **canonical EB activation effects** in the **EB workflow state machine**. EB brings the train to a stop, and the pending cancel then commits in Phase 3. Once `is_stopped()` is true and the cancel latch has been consumed, the operator clears EB through `TrainController.eb.release()`, which transitions the controller to the **`READY` state** per row `ad04` of the EB workflow. This protocol replaces the generic ATO not-running branch for zero-limit rolling trains.
16. `71ee` — After `TrainController.eb.release()` from any state other than `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, transition to the **`READY` state** (per the predicate defined in the Controller state machine subsection). EB activation has already requested cancellation of any live path through `pending_path_cancel_request` (`error.origin = "eb"`), so restart requires fresh path creation by the next eligible Phase 1, which then calls `TrainController.depart()` directly per the canonical depart contract. The controller does not self-resume. `eb.release()` from `RUNNING_AT_STATION` (the dwell EB cycle, see EB workflow row `1bbc`) clears `eb.active` only and stays in `RUNNING_AT_STATION`.
**ATC speed limit**

- ATC restriction selection does not read signal state. CAUTION restriction comes from a single controller-owned global cap, `CAUTION_SPEED_LIMIT_KMH`, applied when `TrainController` samples a block whose computed `Block.state` is `CAUTION`; signals are display state derived from blocks, not an ATC input. `CAUTION_SPEED_LIMIT_KMH` is adjustable as `caution_speed_limit_kmh` in **Runtime settings**.
- In manual mode with a received path plan, the controller lookahead builds the authority-boundary stop so the effective ATC limit enforces the current authority end correctly: it brakes to the `authority_end_block_id` boundary in the current live authority frame using the controller safety margin.
- Treat ATC reason reporting as structured metadata on the lookahead/curve payload, not only as a free-form controller string.

**ATO handle calculation**

- `controller.ato.compute_handle(delta_seconds)` is the per-tick ATO handle selection. The controller calls it once per tick while an ATO run is active and writes the result to `handle_position` (per **Module boundaries** rule 5); it gets its tick delta as the `delta_seconds` argument the controller passes (the same tick delta used elsewhere that tick), not from any stored field.
- `compute_handle(...)` reads the canonical controller `lookahead` property (itself a pure derived value computed live); it does not build a lookahead of its own.
- If the sampled operational speed curve at distance `0` is already at or below the stop threshold, it returns a direct braking notch immediately.
- It selects the notch from a two-point sampling of the operational speed curve:
  - the operational speed curve at distance `0`
  - one future point at a delta-scaled horizon (the lookahead distance grows with current motion using `delta_seconds`, e.g. `speed_cm_s * delta_seconds * LOOKAHEAD_TICKS`)
  - combined with the active ATC and block caps to choose power, coast, or brake.

**Speed curves**

- Build a unified restriction set for the full clearance/authority area and take the minimum of all relevant curves and local limits at each sample point.
- Carry source metadata on each `lookahead.speed_curves` datapoint, such as `atc_limit_reason_code` and `atc_limit_reason_detail`.
- Preserve metadata for which restriction category produced the current minimum ATC limit so monitor/debug UI can explain the displayed number.

**Driver/operator inputs**

- `TrainController.commands.set_handle()` is the active manual-driving command for `simulation` submode manual control. The command may be accepted while manual mode is active, `manual_mode_type == simulation`, the train is powered, and a cab is selected; the effective command is still clamped by ATC lookahead — and fully overridden by EB while `eb.active` — before physics receives it, and the command never clears `eb.active`. `set_handle()` is rejected (`success=False`) when `manual_mode_type == power_percent`.
- `TrainController.commands.set_manual_power_percent()` is the active driver input for `power_percent` submode manual control; the matching API endpoint/client method and train/editor UI control deliver operator input through `manual.set_power_percent(...)`. It accepts under the same manual-input conditions as `set_handle()` except `manual.manual_mode_type` must be `power_percent`: manual mode active, train powered, and cab selected. Like handle input, the command may be accepted before depart has been accepted (and while `eb.active`), but ATC and EB remain authoritative and apply the **Canonical power-percent safety-cap ordering**. Setting `0` while `eb.active == True` is how the driver satisfies the **driver-control interlock** on `TrainController.eb.release()`. The command clamps to `0..100` and does not start movement by itself. It is rejected when `manual_mode_type == simulation` or any shared acceptance precondition fails.
- `TrainController.depart()` is called directly by `RouteExecutor` whenever live authority exists for the current target, including one-off operator routes created from direct UI/API movement requests. No other component must synthesize departure acceptance by other means.

**Dispatcher/route-executor inputs**

- There is no `receive_path_plan(path_plan)` setter, cache refresh, or atomic replacement API in v1. `received_path_plan` is a read-only property that resolves the active dispatcher-owned path into the canonical **received_path_plan schema** on each read; updates to the dispatcher's `Path` registry surface automatically without any controller-side write.
- `received_path_plan` must follow the canonical **received_path_plan schema**. Its nested authority geometry must follow the canonical **resource_sequence entry schema** and **path_tile entry schema**; the controller must not be asked to recover terminal-frame direction later from tile coordinates alone.
- In v1, `received_path_plan` carries the authorized corridor and current authority boundary, not dispatcher-only requested-target fields. The live stop the controller must brake to on this tick is derived from `authority_end_block_id`, the terminal frame in `resource_sequence`, and the controller safety margin.
- `received_path_plan` must not carry a second lifecycle status enum. For the controller, movement authority is binary: `received_path_plan is not None` means there is live authority, and `received_path_plan is None` means there is not.
- `on_physics_updated(span_update)` is called by `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` after it computes the next span/speed and tile delta. `TrainController.on_physics_updated(span_update)` applies the update to controller-owned `current_span` / `current_speed_kmh`. It then builds the canonical progress report from the tile delta, the live `lookahead` (computed from the just-updated span/speed), and `received_path_plan.resource_sequence` when live authority exists, and appends that report to the controller-owned per-train progress mailbox (`physics_sync.queued_progress_reports`). `TrainController.on_physics_updated(...)` samples the appended `ControllerProgressReport.event_time` itself, via `time.time()`, at the moment the report is built (per **Time sampling** under the **Units contract**); the caller does not pass it in. `SimulationController` drains and clears that mailbox in Phase 3 by calling `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)` in order. This method owns span storage and progress interpretation only. It does not mutate dispatcher path records or route state directly.
- `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` is the dwell-entry transition called by the Phase 1 dwell-entry step inside `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)`. There is no `dwell_entry_requested` flag in v1; `RouteExecutor` invokes it directly when `controller.is_recovery_clean` is true and the route's next non-finished task has `task_kind == "dwell"`. The controller call samples the dwell row's `started_at`. The controller call transitions to `RUNNING_AT_STATION`. The controller does not detect dwell entry on its own and does not read `RouteRun`.
- Missing path authority is represented entirely by `received_path_plan is None`. The controller has no `wait_for_path(reason)` command.
- `TrainController.depart()`, `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()`, and `TrainController.finish_dwell()` are controller acceptance/transition commands. The closed list of `TrainController.finish_dwell()` callers in v1 is: (a) the Phase 1 dwell-complete step inside `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` (the canonical dwell-exit caller); (b) `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(...)` when invoked by any of the Phase 3 abort/power-off/deactivate consumers with the controller currently in `RUNNING_AT_STATION`. Both are covered by the **Same-transaction commit-callback exception** in the **Phase handoff rule**. `RouteExecutor` calls `depart()` directly; `enter_station_dwell()` is called only by the Phase 1 dwell-entry step inside `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)`. `TrainController.finish_dwell()` is the end-of-dwell state transition; it lands in the **`READY` state** (per the predicate defined in the Controller state machine subsection), does not clear `received_path_plan`, and does not start movement. Cab selection is preserved across route completion.
- Manual trains use the same Phase 1 dwell-entry step inside `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` as ATO. The difference is departure behavior only: in manual mode `TrainController.depart()` records that the current authority has been accepted and the train is ready to go, but it does not itself start movement or call `TrainController.ato.go()`.
- `TrainController.begin_service_stop()` and `TrainController.consume_service_stop()` are the controller-level accessors for the `ops.service_stop_active` latch; the **Latch Table** defines its writer, consumer, and gating state. `begin_service_stop()` is the latch's canonical writer helper: it is idempotent, valid while powered in either control mode, sets `ops.service_stop_active = True`, and calls `controller.ato.stop()`. It does not cancel authority or write route state, and Controller tick step `b41e` owns the actual per-mode braking override. `consume_service_stop()` clears the latch and changes no other operational state; it is a no-op when the latch is not set, so every caller may call it unconditionally. It emits one `kind = "info"` event through `controller.notifications` only on a call that actually cleared a set latch; a no-op call emits nothing, so the unconditional callers below add no traffic when no service stop was armed. The gating predicate that decides when to consume lives in Phase 1 run step `6a0d`, not inside the accessor: that step consumes only once the train is stopped with no live authority and no pending run-completion release. A run that ends before it reaches that point clears the latch through the same accessor from `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(...)`, `TrainController.complete_power_off()`, and the **canonical EB activation effects**. No other component assigns the field.
- `PathDispatcher` calls `RouteExecutor.handle_path_cancellation(train_id, path_id, error, was_deviation=False, was_run_completion=False)` only after the dispatcher cancel mutation has committed. The ordinary branch records diagnostic context and the deviation-vs-cancel display classification from `was_deviation`; the run-completion branch records no cancellation diagnostic state because step `6a0d` owns that normal progress. The `ErrorRecord` remains diagnostic-only event data.
- `RouteExecutor` must not set controller path or destination fields directly; controller authority is read live by the controller through its `received_path_plan` projection over the dispatcher's `Path` registry.
- `TrainController.eb.release()` clears EB only when the train is stopped and powered, its pending cancel is `None`, `received_path_plan is None`, and the **driver-control interlock** in its own contract is satisfied. From `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, acceptance clears `eb.active` and preserves `RUNNING_AT_STATION`; this is the dwell exception and no route recovery state exists. From every other powered state, acceptance transitions to **`READY`**, after which ordinary Phase-1 cancellation cleanup and fresh planning apply. The method never calls `RouteExecutor`, inspects route target state, or clears route-owned diagnostics.
- After any non-dwell EB stop, the old active path must not be reused. EB activation requests its cancellation; release then clears the EB latch and transitions to `READY`, and fresh authority plus a new `depart()` acceptance are required. The dwell EB cycle is the explicit exception: there was no active path to cancel, release stays in `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, and the ordinary dwell-complete step resumes when eligible.

**`TrainController.depart()` canonical contract**

`TrainController.depart()` is the controller's departure-acceptance command, returning `(success, reason)`. `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` calls it directly on every eligible Phase 1 pass that has live authority for the current target, regardless of `control_mode`; there is no `departure_requested` latch and no route-level departure policy in v1.

- Acceptance preconditions are checked in order; any failure returns `(False, <reason>)` and leaves controller state unchanged, so Phase 1 may retry on a later tick:
  - `received_path_plan is not None` — there must be live authority to depart into.
  - `eb.active == False` — a latched EB blocks departure.
  - `pending_power_off` is not set — a pending shutdown blocks departure.
  - `state ∈ {READY, RUNNING_MOVING}` — the second admits the **Reactive speed-based state evaluation** having already advanced state ahead of this call.
  - In `manual` mode only: a cab is selected (`cab ∈ {A, B}`) and a facing direction is resolved.
- On acceptance the ATO-vs-manual difference is owned inside the call:
  - In `ato` mode, if no cab is selected, `depart()` first selects the cab facing the authority by comparing `received_path_plan.resource_sequence` with `TrainSpan.end_a` / `end_b` (final tie-break: `end_a`); ATO departure never rejects solely for a missing cab. It then delegates to `ato.go()`, the canonical writer of `ato.running`: it sets `ato.running = True` and transitions `READY -> RUNNING_MOVING` (idempotent from `RUNNING_MOVING`).
  - In `manual` mode, a `READY` controller transitions to `RUNNING_MOVING`; a `RUNNING_MOVING` controller is an idempotent success that changes no state.
- The single canonical depart-acceptance signal other layers read is `controller.state == RUNNING_MOVING`, set by any canonical transition into that state in the **Controller state machine** table — either a successful `depart()` call or the **Reactive speed-based state evaluation** observing motion under live authority. The two are equally legitimate; stop-completion eligibility, `ato.running` setting, and route progress all key on `state == RUNNING_MOVING` regardless of which transition fired. Manual driving input may command motion under live authority before Phase 1's `depart()` call lands, and ATC still protects the train (see **Manual mode and movement-authority boundary**).

**route/dispatcher command contracts**

- `TrainController.path_projection.received_path_plan` is a read-only property. On each read, it resolves the dispatcher's currently active `Path` for this train into the canonical **received_path_plan schema** (or `None`). There is no setter and no atomic-replacement entry point. `PathDispatcher` updates its own `Path` record in `create_path(...)` / `extend_path(...)`, and the controller observes those updates on its next read. When the property goes from non-`None` to `None` (because `Path.status` is no longer `ACTIVE`), the controller's `tick()`-start bookkeeping calls `controller.ato.stop()` when it observes the change (authority clear is a run-ending event). That bookkeeping does **not** write `state`: it does not "set" the `RUNNING_MOVING → READY` move. That transition is recomputed every tick by the **Reactive speed-based state evaluation** inside `TrainController.on_physics_updated(...)` from `is_stopped() == True`, no live authority, and `eb.active == False`. Because every cancellation/completion commits only on a stopped train, the train is already at rest by the time `received_path_plan` reads `None`, so the reactive evaluation sees the `READY` conditions and applies the transition itself. v1 holds at most one ACTIVE path per train. `create_path(...)` requires `received_path_plan is None`, and `extend_path(...)` keeps the same `path_id`, so a new `path_id` can never appear while a live path is still present: the old path always transitions to `None` first, the reactive evaluation lands the controller in `READY`, and a fresh path is then accepted from `READY` by a new `depart()` call.
- `TrainController.depart()` follows the single **`TrainController.depart()` canonical contract** immediately above. This command-contract list intentionally does not restate its preconditions, cab selection, or ATO/manual effects.
- `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` is called by the Phase 1 dwell-entry step inside `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)`. Acceptance preconditions: `state == READY`, `eb.active == False`, `cab in {"A", "B"}` (rejected with a diagnostic such as `"cannot enter dwell with no cab selected"` when `cab is None`, enforcing the `RUNNING_AT_STATION` row of **Allowed `(state, cab)` pairs**). The Phase 1 caller additionally enforces that `controller.is_recovery_clean` is true, the route's next non-finished walk-position has `task_kind == "dwell"`, and the predecessor walk-position's `finished_at` is set; these are caller-side gates. This controller call samples the dwell row's `started_at`; it is the only writer of that field. On success, transitions the controller to `RUNNING_AT_STATION` and must not create movement or mutate `received_path_plan`. Returns `(success: bool, reason: Optional[str])`; on rejection the call leaves controller state unchanged and emits one `kind = "warning"` event through `NotificationCenter`.
- `TrainController.finish_dwell()` is valid only from `RUNNING_AT_STATION`. Acceptance preconditions: `state == RUNNING_AT_STATION`. EB latch state is not a precondition; `finish_dwell()` may run with `eb.active == True` because the canonical caller `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(...)` invokes it as part of the Phase-3 power-off / abort / deactivate teardown sequence where EB may still be latched (the OFF cleanup that follows clears `eb.active`). On success, transitions back to the **`READY` state** (per the predicate defined in the Controller state machine subsection), preserves cab selection, does not call `TrainController.depart()`, and does not mutate `received_path_plan`. Returns `(success: bool, reason: Optional[str])`; on rejection the call leaves controller state unchanged and emits one `kind = "warning"` event through `NotificationCenter`. The normal callers are the same train's Phase 1 dwell-complete step inside `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` and `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(...)` invoked by Phase-3 abort/power-off/deactivate consumers.

**Read accessors**

- `get_lookahead()` is the canonical accessor for UI and debug consumers. Any consumer asking for target speed or speed-limit reason must sample this payload at distance `0`.
